# Live issue reconciliation and BNGIR obligation detail (2026-10-19)

## Snapshot and evidence provenance

- Authenticated live `gh issue list --state open --limit 1000 --json number,title,body,state,url,updatedAt`: 43 open issues; downloaded source snapshot SHA256 `1fb6f09d95ee80c8f47b3ec6495e75d7f41e3f58e803cc9134f77b07ff4c5442`. Catalog observation `2026-10-18` has 43 reviewed issue rows, of which all 43 live open issues match exact issue number and body digest; all 3 acceptance criteria on each issue match a criterion verbatim (129/129). The live snapshot file is retained outside the worktree at `/private/tmp/bng3-issue-snapshot-2026-10-19.json`.
- Implementation base for this change is `219c76dac38655c0f15a12ee294e4a4ac10d02ef` (main after #311/#313); it is the base revision, not this change candidate.
- Prior-revision retained evidence only: Cross-tool parity run [39330889076](https://github.com/RuleWorld/BNG3/actions/runs/39330889076), candidate `9af03d79d48c7488a1ffe98ced270053b27478bd`, `dirty=false`, overall workflow conclusion **failure**. Downloaded integrated artifact `bng3-integrated-validation-push-39330889076` (artifact ID `12225344894`, SHA256 `a5690bd8f65775fa6b6ae3275b10a3389ef5eb4bc8bf40233f9bc5c2a04cf3c0`); its `report.json` SHA256 is `b7d322b6387aab96d13e6b10e4c9eeb0edc0bc74b014878e6beeeed036ded07c`. The hosted `bng_cpp` SHA256 was `b0e2f027d44dfd457f4a0a83f4c3f21c00505f03440bcb75a88b8c46480dd37f`. On that prior revision, campaign `bngir-adversarial` selected 18 leaves: 17 PASS and one explicit UNSUPPORTED (`bonded-complex-boundary`); malformed cases were diagnostic-only, not supported execution. These named results do not qualify the current main head. The later main snapshot at `ff36e36bde6015dd7f476e0b3a78a7500eb9e20a` had Cross-tool parity run [39345091931](https://github.com/RuleWorld/BNG3/actions/runs/39345091931) queued when inspected; that is historical too. Exact-head retained evidence for current `219c76da` is pending.
- The paired retained issue queue artifact `bng3-issue-queue-push-39330889076` (artifact ID `12225369672`, SHA256 `c3f085f28c6845ed1bca2846031631deb5a4a55434bdf4e20e24b6d6825219fb`) has `issue-queue.json` SHA256 `aea561cb492785a5162975b5a568b5cec3af5e559749c915d045fdb3e17908bf` and baseline catalog SHA256 `665899c1dee760ff3f954e8b7e2e4423db183f6591a520072093f616ab03a11f`. Its snapshot contains 60 issue rows (43 open plus closed rows); this report restricts shallow reconciliation to the 43 currently open. Exact checks must be joined by check ID; PR/source test prose is not execution evidence.
- The network-rule execution qualification (`tests/validation/netrule_execution.py`) is new in this change. It compares the compiled rule contract, the legacy network-rule path and a hand-derived closed-form law, with the canonical Perl BNG2 network as an external oracle, over single-molecule rule patterns without bonds, compartments or delete-molecules. Its nine named checks are not part of the retained prior-revision report, so the check-state column records them as not selected on that base; the exact-revision run of this change is recorded with the change's acceptance evidence, not in this retained report.
- The identity-contract qualification (`tests/validation/graph_identity_contract.py`) is new in this change. It compares the two native graph encodings (BNGcore's `PatternGraph` canonical label and NFsim's `Complex` canonical label) and the network species identity against the independent validation graph matcher, at the layer each surface declares in the shared identity contract (`cpp/core/IdentityContract.hpp`). The generated families are symmetric rings under bond renumbering and traversal order, per-molecule and species compartment distinctions, and state and topology controls. Its 28 named checks are not part of the retained prior-revision report, so the check-state column records them as not selected on that base; the exact-revision run of this change is recorded with the change's acceptance evidence, not in this
- Separately, current local reduced native run used installed module `/opt/anaconda3/lib/python3.14/site-packages/bionetgen/_bionetgen_cpp.cpython-314-darwin.so`, SHA256 `3c17f1a145ab56dca0c5cf0bd0da21997dee93b8c03cbf2a20c8d8b26c9d53af`. It proves only that these checks pass against that installed CPython 3.14 binary; source revision is not tied to the candidate. Hosted re-execution remains required.

## Shallow reconciliation of all 43 open issues

The existing catalog is the exact source for acceptance text, scoped behavior, dependencies, check expectations/IDs, test references, completion conditions, and human decisions. The table classifies each issue across implementation/behavior, executable contract, evidence, optional expansion, and governance. `qualification:*` rows without generated leaves are catalog-to-execution mapping gaps; generic expectations do not specify a behavioral check. `scope splits` count distinct bounded obligations under one live acceptance sentence, not a claim that the live acceptance sentence was rewritten. Optional-boundary flags are a conservative keyword locator in scope/boundary fields, not a support decision. Exact compound criteria are printed with every distinct catalog scope beneath them; one-scope criteria may still need wording review. Human decisions and missing check contracts are separate categories. Current catalog totals: 210 obligations, 161 named runnable checks, 73 manual disposition entries, 302 scope-reconciliation notes, and 5 explicit human decisions. The current dashboard run executed no new report; the exact check-state column below uses the retained prior-revision 9af report, not mere check selection.

The comparison tolerance is 1e-9 relative for the coefficient, the right-hand side and the propensity, and 1e-7 relative for the ODE trajectory control, which is the error budget of the numerical solver.; The declared supported slice: single-reaction rules over free, non-compartment molecules with no filter, no rule modifier, no energy pattern and no population map; rate laws that resolve to an elementary parameter rate, a Saturation or Hill special rate, a two-reactant MichaelisMenten special rate, a table function, or a model function whose only argument is a local molecule scope.; The independent reference is a closed-form law written in the harness: no comparison reads a value the engine produced from the same quantity the expectation is derived from.; This finite slice does not satisfy the other feature combinations in the broad acceptance criterion. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; The Lean reference (formal/lean/BNG/Ids.lean and formal/lean/BNG/NameResolution.lean) states the same name-to-id correspondence in the formal model; the executable fixture does not re-prove those theorems and the two artifacts stay distinct.; The correspondence is stated in three parts: the compiled ids of a namespace are dense (0..n-1) and follow the declaration order of that namespace; every serialized id reference denotes the declaration the serialization names beside it; and the BNGIR v0.2 model section is exactly the resolved model's compiled snapshot.; The declared domain is the generated grammar: models whose declarations are drawn from the parameter, compartment, molecule-type, function, observable, seed-species and reaction-rule blocks, with explicit site states and with no energy pattern, barrier pattern or population map.; The executable fixture and the Lean kernel-verified statements are separate artifacts: a passing fixture never discharges a Lean obligation.; The expectation is the identity table the generator wrote before the compiler ran, so a defect in the compiler's id assignment appears as a difference against an expectation the compiler never produced.; The selected refinement statement is that the compiled-id assignment refines the name-keyed declaration correspondence: within a namespace two declarations share an id exactly when they share a name, and every serialized reference resolves to exactly one declaration, so the serialized identities determine the resolved model uniquely and the resolved model determines the serialized identities uniquely.; The statement is proved executively over the declared domain of the generated grammar; it is not a machine-checked theorem and it says nothing about a model outside that grammar. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; 1 evidence/record scopes | A saturated or Hill law with repeated identical reactants stays outside this ensemble family's slice, but the defect that put it there is repaired and the canonical convention is now known. The statistical factor of identical reactants is a property of the rule's reactants, not of the rate law's shape: canonical Perl BNG2 2.9.3 writes it as a prefix on the rate law for every rate law type, so its own network for `A() + A() -> D() Sat(kcat,K)` reads `0.5*Sat kcat K`, and this engine's own network writer emits the same prefix. The engine used to drop the factor for a Saturation, Hill or Michaelis-Menten law, so it disagreed with the network it exported; the factor is now applied and the engine's ODE reproduces canonical BNG2's trajectory for that model to 2.3e-8 relative (A_tot 15.50050409333 and D_tot 2.249747953337 at t=1 against 15.500504128654386 and 2.2497479356728047). Both of the engine's stochastic backends now carry it: the NFsim route kept the symmetry factor on the transformation set but never applied it to a special rate, so it answered with the factor-free rate while the native route answered with the factor-carrying one, and the answer depended on the backend. The NFsim special rates now apply the symmetry factor exactly as the ordinary base rate does, and the two backends agree within their sampling error (a mean final count of 2.26 and 2.09 over 80 seeded trajectories each, against 3.58 before the repair). Canonical BNG2's own SSA and ODE still disagree with each other for this form: its SSA mean final count is 1.98 (standard error 0.08) over 261 seeded trajectories against its own ODE value of 2.2497, so there is no single canonical ensemble law to reproduce. This engine uses the same coefficient in its ODE and in both of its SSA backends, and the coefficient, right-hand side and propensity of the form are judged by the rate-law contract family (`rate-law-contract:special-multiplicity-r*:coefficient|rhs|propensity`) and the backend agreement by `tests/python/test_special_rate_statistical_factor.py` instead of by an ensemble comparison against canonical BNG2's SSA digits.; One generated case of the named convention, compared with the independent count law of that convention under the recorded seed policy and run budget.; The comparison is distributional over the recorded ensemble under a fixed seed policy and a fixed run budget; it is not a pathwise claim and it never compares one backend against another.; The qualified slice states the convention of one distinct reactant (the elementary parameter, a local function, a builtin call, a TFUN table, the Sat and Hill special rates) and the elementary bimolecular rule with two identical reactants; every other rate convention stays outside the slice.; The reference is the analytic law; no deterministic solver output is used, so the statement claims nothing about the floating-point accuracy of a solver.; The selected refinement statement is that the stochastic ensemble mean is the analytic mean of the declared law at every sampled time, within the recorded statistical error budget; the Lean kernel-verification theorem statements are not selected by this check and stay unqualified. | 5 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; Missing required qualification ID: qualification:issue:167:closure-record.; +3 catalogued notes | No multi-scope criterion; broad phrasing still requires review against named checks and catalog scope. |

Reaction rates, symmetry factors, stoichiometry and observable weights are outside this family and stay unqualified here.; The declared domain is: at least one and at most four reaction rules, at most three seed species of which no two are isomorphic, no dangling bond in a seed species, at most eight species and twelve reactions per network, and at most twelve molecule and site vertices per species.; The equivalence is claimed for the declared domain only; a case outside it is refused rather than passed.; The independent construction is the canonical Perl BNG2 network for the same source, read by the harness's own reader and compared with the harness's own labeled-graph matcher, so the comparison never depends on how either implementation spells a species.; The intended bounded equivalence is that the production network's species and the canonical implementation's species are in bijection under the independent graph-isomorphism relation, and that the isomorphism relation between the two networks is exactly that correspondence: every species has exactly one partner and neither network keeps two species the independent matcher calls identical.; The termination argument: both generators are bounded (the canonical implementation stops when an iteration adds no species and the production generator stops after at most 100 rule-application iterations), the harness re-checks the size bounds after both networks exist and refuses a case that exceeds them, and the comparison is a bipartite matching over at most eight species per side. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; Missing required qualification ID: qualification:issue:168:generate-differential-cases-comparing-production-graph-identity.; +3 catalogued notes | No multi-scope criterion; broad phrasing still requires review against named checks and catalog scope. |

The Lean reference (formal/lean/BNG/Ids.lean and formal/lean/BNG/NameResolution.lean) states the same name-to-id correspondence in the formal model; the executable fixture does not re-prove those theorems and the two artifacts stay distinct.; The correspondence is stated in three parts: the compiled ids of a namespace are dense (0..n-1) and follow the declaration order of that namespace; every serialized id reference denotes the declaration the serialization names beside it; and the BNGIR v0.2 model section is exactly the resolved model's compiled snapshot.; The declared domain is the generated grammar: models whose declarations are drawn from the parameter, compartment, molecule-type, function, observable, seed-species and reaction-rule blocks, with explicit site states and with no energy pattern, barrier pattern or population map.; The executable fixture and the Lean kernel-verified statements are separate artifacts: a passing fixture never discharges a Lean obligation.; The expectation is the identity table the generator wrote before the compiler ran, so a defect in the compiler's id assignment appears as a difference against an expectation the compiler never produced.; The selected refinement statement is that the compiled-id assignment refines the name-keyed declaration correspondence: within a namespace two declarations share an id exactly when they share a name, and every serialized reference resolves to exactly one declaration, so the serialized identities determine the resolved model uniquely and the resolved model determines the serialized identities uniquely.; The statistical factor of identical reactants is a property of the rule's reactants, not of the rate law's shape: canonical Perl BNG2 2.9.3 writes it as a prefix on the rate law for every rate law type, so its own network for `A() + A() -> D() Sat(kcat,K)` reads `0.5*Sat kcat K`, and this engine's own network writer emits the same prefix. The engine used to drop the factor for a Saturation, Hill or Michaelis-Menten law, so it disagreed with the network it exported; the factor is now applied and the engine's ODE reproduces canonical BNG2's trajectory for that model to 2.3e-8 relative (A_tot 15.50050409333 and D_tot 2.249747953337 at t=1 against 15.500504128654386 and 2.2497479356728047). Both of the engine's stochastic backends now carry it: the NFsim route kept the symmetry factor on the transformation set but never applied it to a special rate, so it answered with the factor-free rate while the native route answered with the factor-carrying one, and the answer depended on the backend. The NFsim special rates now apply the symmetry factor exactly as the ordinary base rate does, and the two backends agree within their sampling error (a mean final count of 2.26 and 2.09 over 80 seeded trajectories each, against 3.58 before the repair). Canonical BNG2's own SSA and ODE still disagree with each other for this form: its SSA mean final count is 1.98 (standard error 0.08) over 261 seeded trajectories against its own ODE value of 2.2497, so there is no single canonical ensemble law to reproduce. This engine uses the same coefficient in its ODE and in both of its SSA backends, and the coefficient, right-hand side and propensity of the form are judged by the rate-law contract family (`rate-law-contract:special-multiplicity-r*:coefficient|rhs|propensity`) and the backend agreement by `tests/python/test_special_rate_statistical_factor.py` instead of by an ensemble comparison against canonical BNG2's SSA digits.; One generated case of the named convention, compared with the independent count law of that convention under the recorded seed policy and run budget.; The comparison is distributional over the recorded ensemble under a fixed seed policy and a fixed run budget; it is not a pathwise claim and it never compares one backend against another.; The qualified slice states the convention of one distinct reactant (the elementary parameter, a local function, a builtin call, a TFUN table, the Sat and Hill special rates) and the elementary bimolecular rule with two identical reactants; every other rate convention stays outside the slice.; The reference is the analytic law; no deterministic solver output is used, so the statement claims nothing about the floating-point accuracy of a solver.; The selected refinement statement is that the stochastic ensemble mean is the analytic mean of the declared law at every sampled time, within the recorded statistical error budget; the Lean kernel-verification theorem statements are not selected by this check and stay unqualified. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; Missing required qualification ID: qualification:issue:167:encode-the-exact-supported-special-rate-local-function-tfun-and.; +3 catalogued notes | No multi-scope criterion; broad phrasing still requires review against named checks and catalog scope. |

Reaction rates, symmetry factors, stoichiometry and observable weights are outside this family and stay unqualified here.; The declared domain is: at least one and at most four reaction rules, at most three seed species of which no two are isomorphic, no dangling bond in a seed species, at most eight species and twelve reactions per network, and at most twelve molecule and site vertices per species.; The equivalence is claimed for the declared domain only; a case outside it is refused rather than passed.; The independent construction is the canonical Perl BNG2 network for the same source, read by the harness's own reader and compared with the harness's own labeled-graph matcher, so the comparison never depends on how either implementation spells a species.; The intended bounded equivalence is that the production network's species and the canonical implementation's species are in bijection under the independent graph-isomorphism relation, and that the isomorphism relation between the two networks is exactly that correspondence: every species has exactly one partner and neither network keeps two species the independent matcher calls identical.; The termination argument: both generators are bounded (the canonical implementation stops when an iteration adds no species and the production generator stops after at most 100 rule-application iterations), the harness re-checks the size bounds after both networks exist and refuses a case that exceeds them, and the comparison is a bipartite matching over at most eight species per side. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; Missing required qualification ID: qualification:issue:168:generate-differential-cases-comparing-production-graph-identity.; +3 catalogued notes | No multi-scope criterion; broad phrasing still requires review against named checks and catalog scope. |

The correspondence is stated in three parts: the compiled ids of a namespace are dense (0..n-1) and follow the declaration order of that namespace; every serialized id reference denotes the declaration the serialization names beside it; and the BNGIR v0.2 model section is exactly the resolved model's compiled snapshot.; The declared domain is the generated grammar: models whose declarations are drawn from the parameter, compartment, molecule-type, function, observable, seed-species and reaction-rule blocks, with explicit site states and with no energy pattern, barrier pattern or population map.; The executable fixture and the Lean kernel-verified statements are separate artifacts: a passing fixture never discharges a Lean obligation.; The expectation is the identity table the generator wrote before the compiler ran, so a defect in the compiler's id assignment appears as a difference against an expectation the compiler never produced.; The selected refinement statement is that the compiled-id assignment refines the name-keyed declaration correspondence: within a namespace two declarations share an id exactly when they share a name, and every serialized reference resolves to exactly one declaration, so the serialized identities determine the resolved model uniquely and the resolved model determines the serialized identities uniquely.; The statement is proved executively over the declared domain of the generated grammar; it is not a machine-checked theorem and it says nothing about a model outside that grammar. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; The statistical factor of identical reactants is a property of the rule's reactants, not of the rate law's shape: canonical Perl BNG2 2.9.3 writes it as a prefix on the rate law for every rate law type, so its own network for `A() + A() -> D() Sat(kcat,K)` reads `0.5*Sat kcat K`, and this engine's own network writer emits the same prefix. The engine used to drop the factor for a Saturation, Hill or Michaelis-Menten law, so it disagreed with the network it exported; the factor is now applied and the engine's ODE reproduces canonical BNG2's trajectory for that model to 2.3e-8 relative (A_tot 15.50050409333 and D_tot 2.249747953337 at t=1 against 15.500504128654386 and 2.2497479356728047). Both of the engine's stochastic backends now carry it: the NFsim route kept the symmetry factor on the transformation set but never applied it to a special rate, so it answered with the factor-free rate while the native route answered with the factor-carrying one, and the answer depended on the backend. The NFsim special rates now apply the symmetry factor exactly as the ordinary base rate does, and the two backends agree within their sampling error (a mean final count of 2.26 and 2.09 over 80 seeded trajectories each, against 3.58 before the repair). Canonical BNG2's own SSA and ODE still disagree with each other for this form: its SSA mean final count is 1.98 (standard error 0.08) over 261 seeded trajectories against its own ODE value of 2.2497, so there is no single canonical ensemble law to reproduce. This engine uses the same coefficient in its ODE and in both of its SSA backends, and the coefficient, right-hand side and propensity of the form are judged by the rate-law contract family (`rate-law-contract:special-multiplicity-r*:coefficient|rhs|propensity`) and the backend agreement by `tests/python/test_special_rate_statistical_factor.py` instead of by an ensemble comparison against canonical BNG2's SSA digits.; One generated case of the named convention, compared with the independent count law of that convention under the recorded seed policy and run budget.; The comparison is distributional over the recorded ensemble under a fixed seed policy and a fixed run budget; it is not a pathwise claim and it never compares one backend against another.; The qualified slice states the convention of one distinct reactant (the elementary parameter, a local function, a builtin call, a TFUN table, the Sat and Hill special rates) and the elementary bimolecular rule with two identical reactants; every other rate convention stays outside the slice.; The reference is the analytic law; no deterministic solver output is used, so the statement claims nothing about the floating-point accuracy of a solver.; The selected refinement statement is that the stochastic ensemble mean is the analytic mean of the declared law at every sampled time, within the recorded statistical error budget; the Lean kernel-verification theorem statements are not selected by this check and stay unqualified. | 7 items: Execute a retained exact-revision check for this scope.; Missing required qualification ID: qualification:issue:167:encode-the-exact-supported-special-rate-local-function-tfun-and.; +3 catalogued notes | No multi-scope criterion; broad phrasing still requires review against named checks and catalog scope. |

Compound/ambiguity rule: where one acceptance sentence has multiple catalog scopes, the table prints the exact split terms and each scope. A broad criterion with only one scope may still need human wording review. Qualification placeholders expose a detection-mapping gap, not absent product functionality. The optional-limit column is a conservative keyword locator; consult exact boundary strings in the catalog before making a support claim.

## Explicitly under-specified or ambiguous requirements

- **#153 reconstruction/round-trip:** “supported resolved model” and source-text independence need a stated supported model subset plus observable equality criteria. The four retained finite cases demonstrate their listed structure/seed/RHS/trajectory only; export, general graphs, and migration remain separate.
- **#154 versioned feature coverage:** declarations, IDs, reversible directions, filters/scopes, dimensions, compartments, energy, population metadata, and protocol need a version-by-feature preservation/refusal matrix and an independent semantic oracle. A schema-shape pass alone is insufficient.
- **#155 export:** supported exporter subset, which consumer reimports, and equality relation are unspecified. **Deterministic serialization:** canonical bytes versus semantics-only equality, declaration ordering, and provenance fields need separate rules. **Migration:** source/destination version, unit/population limits, and refusal conditions need named fixtures. **Generated property cases:** finite deterministic controls do not define property generator domain or budget.
- **#156 semantic equality:** specify whether identity, provenance, declaration order, and cross-version migrations participate in equality. The public-default/migration choice remains a maintainer decision, not an automated check.

## Proposed issue #155 correction (draft; live issue untouched)

Keep the exact current criterion on a parent tracking issue, and split its compound acceptance into these independently verifiable bullets:
- **Import** — import independently authored schema-valid documents directly through the native BNGIR reader; compare resolved semantics without BNGL render/parse fallback and retain positive controls/refusals.
- **Export and serialization** — independently round-trip supported models and verify deterministic serialization under declaration-order and identity/provenance controls.
- **Version migration** — exercise explicit v0.1/v0.2 fixtures; refuse any feature the destination version cannot preserve and state unit/population limitations.
- **Generated reference refusal** — generate schema-valid invalid-index and name/ID-mismatch controls for seed, rule-reactant, and rule-product molecule-type references; import directly through native BNGIR; compare with an independently authored positive control. Missing importer is `HARNESS_ERROR`/`NOT_TESTED`; explicit refusal is diagnostic PASS, never supported semantic execution. Four new deterministic rule-side controls do not establish arbitrary property-based coverage, export determinism, migration, or complete round-trips.

## The three planned tasks, and what they became

1. **#155 export and deterministic serialization** — planned `bngir-export:v02-independent-roundtrip`
   and `bngir-export:canonical-bytes-order-invariance`. Delivered as
   `bngir-export:v02-independent-roundtrip`, `bngir-export:v02-site-bearing-roundtrip`,
   `bngir-export:v02-site-bearing-python-roundtrip` and
   `bngir-export:canonical-bytes-determinism`. Each names an independent consumer: the
   native importer, the Python reader and writer, and the retained canonical bytes.
2. **#153/#154 reconstruction, semantics, and version refusal** — planned
   `bngir-reconstruction:source-text-independent-v02` and
   `bngir-version-migration:v01-v02-feature-matrix`. Delivered together with
   `bngir-reconstruction:v02-scopes-filters-and-mutations`,
   `bngir-reconstruction:v02-observables-and-compartments` and
   `bngir-version-migration:v01-v02-refusal-ownership`. The matrix covers 22 feature
   cells; each either preserves the feature or is refused with an owning requirement.
3. **#156 maintainer policy and semantic equality** — the human decision was recorded
   (keep v0.1 as the public default, require an explicit v0.2 opt-in, refuse unknown
   versions) and is held on the obligation, outside the automated repair queue.
   `bngir-equality:v01-v02-identity-provenance` and
   `bngir-equality:approved-default-v01-explicit-v02` assert the recorded contract.

## #155/#156 export, determinism, and equality detector

Implemented in `tests/validation/bngir_format_contracts.py`, selected by the existing
`bngir-adversarial` integrated operation, and reported through the existing report and
investigation-packet paths. Five checks replace the four unmapped `qualification:*`
placeholders for #155's import/export, migration, and serialization obligations, and two of
#156's obligations:

- `bngir-export:v02-independent-roundtrip` — native import and export of the retained
  site-free v0.2 document (`tests/fixtures/bngir/v0.2-site-free-version-bridge.json`)
  reproduces it exactly. This check executes; it is not a fixed unsupported outcome.
- `bngir-export:v02-site-bearing-unsupported` — native import of the retained site-bearing
  v0.2 document is explicitly refused (`must be an ordinary site-free type`). Recorded as an
  explicit unsupported boundary, not as supported execution.
- `bngir-export:canonical-bytes-determinism` — repeated v0.2 serialization is byte-identical
  and equals the frozen canonical v0.2 wire bytes.
- `bngir-equality:v01-v02-identity-provenance` — `semantic_equal` accepts the v0.1-to-v0.2
  round trip and rejects a changed model identity. Provenance is outside `semantic_equal`'s
  model API and is stated as such rather than implied.
- `bngir-equality:approved-default-v01-explicit-v02` — the recorded maintainer decision holds:
  v0.1 is the public default, v0.2 requires explicit opt-in, and unknown versions are refused.

The #156 default/migration decision is recorded on the obligation itself
(`issue:156:decide-the-public-default-version-and-supported-migration-policy`), outside the
automated repair queue; the named check only guards that decision against drift.

Detection evidence: a controlled perturbation of the exported document makes
`bngir-export:v02-independent-roundtrip` report `FAIL` with the first difference, and the
valid controls still pass. Missing execution and the site-bearing unsupported boundary stay
explicit. Hosted exact-candidate execution remains pending.

Remaining: none for #155. The export contract now covers the site-free envelope natively and
the site-bearing envelope through the Python reader; the native refusal of site-bearing
molecule types stays an explicit boundary owned by
`issue:153:reconstruct-the-supported-resolved-model-without-rendering-or-pa`.

## #153/#154 reconstruction and version-migration detector

Implemented in `tests/validation/bngir_reconstruction_contracts.py`, selected by the existing
`bngir-adversarial` integrated operation, and reported through the existing report and
investigation-packet paths.

- `bngir-reconstruction:source-text-independent-v02` — an independently authored v0.2 wire
  document (three parameters, three molecule types, two seeds, two rules) reconstructs a BNGL
  source model through the native reader with BNGL render and parse disabled. Its seed, ODE
  right-hand side and trajectory match the BNGL source and the hand-derived expectation
  (`A()=100, B()=40, C()=0`; `dA/dt=-10, dB/dt=2, dC/dt=8`).
- `bngir-reconstruction:v02-scopes-filters-and-mutations` — the native reader has an explicit
  disposition for the v0.2 rule features it may not support, and each disposition names the
  requirement that owns it:

| probe | native disposition | owning requirement |
|---|---|---|
| `rule-filters` | SUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |
| `rule-local-scopes` | SUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |
| `rule-mutations` | SUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |

- `bngir-reconstruction:v02-observables-and-compartments` — the native reader has an explicit
  disposition for the v0.2 observable and compartment surface:

| probe | native disposition | owning requirement |
|---|---|---|
| `observable-molecules` | SUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |
| `observable-species` | SUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |
| `compartments` | SUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |
| `compartmented-seed-pattern` | UNSUPPORTED | `issue:154:cover-declarations-ids-reversible-directions-mutations-scopes-fi` |

- `bngir-version-migration:v01-v02-feature-matrix` — twenty-two v0.1 feature cells migrate to
  v0.2. Every cell is either preserved or rejected. A feature that disappears without a
  rejection is a silent loss, and the check fails unless the loss is recorded in the
  obligation catalog with an owning requirement. A recorded gap that no longer reproduces also
  fails, so the catalog cannot silently go stale.
- `bngir-version-migration:v01-v02-refusal-ownership` — every cell that the migration does not
  preserve names the requirement that owns that boundary and retains its own v0.1 and v0.2
  evidence. A boundary without a resolvable owner fails the check.

### Matrix cells and dispositions

| `barrier-patterns` | PRESERVED | `issue:154:check-semantic-execution-as-well-as-document-shape-reject-every` | `barrier-patterns.v02.json` |
| `population-maps` | PRESERVED | `issue:154:check-semantic-execution-as-well-as-document-shape-reject-every` | `population-maps.v02.json` |

| `protocol-actions` | PRESERVED | `issue:154:check-semantic-execution-as-well-as-document-shape-reject-every` | `protocol-actions.v02.json` |
| `versioned-features` | PRESERVED | `issue:153:round-trip-and-execute-reconstructed-models-with-source-text-ind` | `versioned-features.v02.json` |

All twenty-two cells preserve the feature and import natively. No cell loses a feature
silently and no cell is rejected without an explicit reason: the migration, the v0.1 reader
and the native reader all fail closed with a message that names the unsupported feature.

16 defects were found and repaired while the matrix was extended, and their records were
removed from the obligation catalog:

- The v0.2 writer silently dropped a rule's non-empty `driving_work`, because the v0.2 rule
  format had no field for it. The v0.2 rule format now carries the work as a resolved
  expression tree, and the migration preserves it instead of refusing it.
- The v0.2 writer silently dropped model-scope protocol actions, because the compiled
  snapshot only carried simulation-protocol actions. The snapshot now carries both scopes.
- The v0.1 reader rendered the parameter actions with a named-argument hash, which the
  grammar only accepts for the other action commands. It now emits the positional form those
  actions use and keeps the named-argument hash for the rest.
- A v0.1 document carrying barrier patterns failed with an internal snapshot error. The v0.1
  reader now rebuilds a barrier pattern, and every v0.1 document is checked against the
  compiler before it is accepted, so an invalid model is refused where it is read instead of
  failing later.
- The native reader refused reversible rule directions and keyword rule modifiers. Both are
  now natively supported, and the native reader fails closed on a reverse direction carried by
  a forward rule and on a modifier that is not an object with a known kind.
- The native reader refused substance-unit metadata that the native model can carry. The
  refusal is removed and the round trip is asserted.
- The native reader refused population molecule types that the native model can carry. The
  population flag now reaches the native molecule type and the import is asserted.
- The native reader refused protocol actions that the native model can carry. The importer
  takes the protocol section and adds each action to the model or the simulation protocol.
- The native reader refused energy patterns that the native model can carry. The importer
  lowers each energy pattern to the same expression and graph the compiler uses, with the
  pattern text retained as provenance.
- The native reader refused rule filters that the native model can carry. The importer builds
  the include/exclude modifier text the native rule parser understands.
- The native reader refused site-bearing molecule types and the patterns that use them. The
  importer now builds the molecule type's components and lowers each site to the component
  node the parser's own pattern builder produces, with the state constraint and the bond. The
  native export contract now covers the site-bearing envelope as well as the site-free one.
- The native reader refused a rule's state change. The document's state change is now checked
  against the product pattern's site state, because the native rule derives the change from
  the reactant and product patterns.
- The native reader refused rule local scopes, because the native rule collected them from
  pattern text the wire format does not carry. The rule now declares its local scopes
  structurally, the compiler prefers the declaration over the pattern text, and the compiled
  snapshot emits the scopes again.
- The v0.1 wire format carried a population map without its rate, so the reader could not
  rebuild the map the engine needs. The rate is now part of the v0.1 population map, and a
  map that still states no rate is refused with a message that names the missing rate.
- The v0.2 schema for population maps still encoded the pre-canonical rate-less vocabulary,
  so the emitter's own output did not validate against it. The schema now states the
  canonical `population`/`population_id`/`rate` keys and keeps the legacy `function` alias as
  an optional field, and the barrier section states the transition's structured patterns and
  a resolved expression tree.
- The native reader refused a barrier pattern, because the wire form carried only the
  transition's text and not the structured patterns the engine needs. The barrier now carries
  its structured patterns and a resolved expression tree, the transition's text is retained as
  provenance so the document round-trips exactly, and the reaction centre is cross-checked.

No cell remains an explicit unsupported boundary: every feature class the matrix
covers is now preserved through the migration and imported natively. The boundaries that
remained after each individual repair are recorded on the obligations they belong to, and
the native reader still refuses a barrier pattern whose transition does not reduce to one
supported reaction centre, because such a barrier has no centre to contribute to.

### Issue 141 CVODE cleanup

The ASan job's native regression, `OdeIntegrator recovers after late stop condition
failure`, detects a real leak: a stop-condition failure raised after CVODE has allocated
its state left the solver, the state vector, the linear solver, the matrix and the
SUNDIALS context allocated. The CVODE path now releases every resource through one
guard, so an exception on any exit path releases the same set the normal path does, in
the same order. The functional recovery and the full ASan build pass locally; the leak
detection itself needs the Linux ASan runner, because LeakSanitizer is not available on
the local platform.

### Issue 141 compile-once / instantiate-many qualification

The lifecycle harness in `tests/validation/integrated_protocol.py` now names one
executable check per remaining #141 obligation. Each check runs over the declared
four-case generated slice (`v1-n20-r0-a0`, `reversible-v3-n80-r0-a0`,
`dimerization-v1-n20-r0-a0`, `dimerization-v3-n80-r0-a0`) and compares against an
independent expectation, never against the instance under test:

- `lifecycle-repeated-instantiation` — one compiled model instantiated three times;
  every deterministic and every seeded trajectory equals a fresh compilation.
- `lifecycle-concurrent-instantiation` — four concurrently constructed and simulated
  instances; every seeded trajectory equals a fresh compilation with the same seed,
  equal seeds agree, different seeds differ, and the generated-network caches are
  distinct objects.
- `lifecycle-independent-rng` — an interleaved replay of one seed reproduces its
  trajectory exactly, a second seed produces a different trajectory, and an
  independently constructed instance reproduces the same seed.
- `lifecycle-independent-caches` — one instance mutated while its sibling keeps its own
  parameter values and its own generated-network object.
- `lifecycle-independent-solver-state` — repeated, early-stopped and interleaved
  stochastic runs on one instance leave the deterministic trajectory unchanged.
- `lifecycle-exception-cleanup` — a stop-condition failure raised after the solver
  allocated its state leaves the instance usable and every recovered trajectory equal
  to a fresh compilation.
- `lifecycle-parameter-only-reuse` — a rate-parameter override preserves the compiled
  structure and reproduces a fresh compilation and the closed-form law.
- `lifecycle-topology-invalidation` — a topology-changing `include_model` action
  invalidates the cached network; the rebuilt trajectory equals a fresh compilation of
  the merged source and the closed-form chain law.
- `lifecycle-compartment-invalidation` — a `setVolume` action invalidates reuse; the
  rebuilt trajectory equals a fresh compilation with the new volume and the closed-form
  volume law.

The topology and compartment checks exposed a product defect: `BioNetGenModel.execute()`
applied the model's actions to the compiled model but left the cached generated network
in place, so an action that changed the model was followed by a simulation of the old
network. `python/bionetgen/model.py` now discards the cached network when the action
block runs, in a `finally` clause so a failed action cannot leave a stale cache either.
The regressions are `tests/python/test_model_reuse_contract.py::test_action_execution_invalidates_the_cached_generated_network`
and `::test_volume_action_invalidates_the_cached_generated_network`; both fail against
the unrepaired model and pass against the repaired one.

The catalog's nine #141 obligations now name these checks instead of the
`qualification:issue:141:*` placeholders, and their scope boundaries and feature
combinations are declared. Runtime topology editing through the public `Model` API
remains unsupported and stays an explicit boundary. The governance obligations
(`implementation-or-disposition`, `exact-revision`, `validation-receipt`,
`remaining-limits`) keep their disposition checks and are still open, so the issue
stays open.

### Export contract scope

`bngir-export:v02-site-bearing-roundtrip` exercises the site-bearing v0.2 envelope through
the native reader and writer, and `bngir-export:v02-site-bearing-python-roundtrip` exercises
the same envelope through the Python reader and writer. Both reproduce the retained document
exactly. The site-free envelope has its own contract, `bngir-export:v02-independent-roundtrip`,
so the two envelopes stay separately visible.

### JAX backend on an accelerator

The CI job for the JAX backend pins `JAX_PLATFORMS: cpu` so its result is deterministic
and independent of the accelerator a runner happens to have. The same suites were run
locally on the Apple Metal device of this machine, which the CI job cannot reach:

- `JAX_ENABLE_X64=false` with the default Metal device: `tests/python/test_jax_ode.py` and
  `tests/python/test_jax_ssa.py`, 25 passed.
- `JAX_ENABLE_X64=true` with the default Metal device: `tests/python/test_jax_ode.py`, 20
  passed.

The device is reported by JAX as `METAL`; the lower-case spelling `metal` is rejected by
JAX's own backend list, so an accelerator run must use the name JAX reports. This is
supplementary device-diversity evidence for the JAX backend, not a replacement for the
CPU-pinned gate.

### Energy extension qualification (#177)

`tests/validation/energy_qualification.py` qualifies the barrier and reservoir energy
extensions on a fixed generated corpus of two-state energy models plus one three-state
driven cycle. Every rate claim is compared against the closed-form law of the case, never
against a number the engine produced:

- `detailed-balance` compares the resolved forward and reverse rate constants with the
  analytic Arrhenius pair `exp[-(Ea+B+phi(dG-W))]` and `exp[-(Ea+B+(1-phi)(W-dG))]`.
- `transition-affinity` compares the effective free-energy difference implied by the rate
  ratio with the analytic `dG-W`, and compares the case with a barrier-free twin so the
  barrier's contribution to the affinity is shown to be zero.
- `reversal` checks the negated effective difference of the reverse traversal and that
  the mirrored rule reproduces the mirrored analytic pair.
- `units` checks that the rate constants are homogeneous of degree one in the declared
  energy, so the declared energies are in RT units and no other conversion applies.
- `network-free-agreement` runs the network-free NFsim ensemble and compares it with the
  same closed-form two-state law the network path was checked against; this is the
  differential control between the two reservoir-work conventions.
- `exporter-semantics` checks that the BNGL, BNG-XML and NET exports carry the extension
  and round trip into the same rate constants, and that SBML, MATLAB and LaTeX refuse
  explicitly and name the construct.
- `independent-reference` runs the canonical Perl BNG2 oracle on the plain eBNGL
  Arrhenius subset, evaluates BNG2's own symbolic rate expansion with the harness's own
  evaluator, and compares the numbers with the product's resolved constants.

The canonical oracle cannot express the barrier or reservoir extension: it aborts with
`could not process block type 'barrier patterns'`. That limitation is a named boundary,
not a silent gap: the extension subset is judged against the analytic law alone, and the
canonical oracle is used only for the subset it implements.

The extension stays experimental and opt-in. With `BNG_NFSIM_GENERAL_ENERGY` unset, the
network writer, the ODE/SSA path and the network-free NFsim path all refuse the model and
name the gate; an explicit `0` keeps the gate off. Two product defects were found and
repaired while building this evidence:

1. The BNGL writer emitted `arrhenius(...)`, `sat(...)`, `hill(...)` and `mm(...)` -- the
   lower-cased spellings the parser itself produces -- and the parser reads those back as
   ordinary identifiers, so a model BNG3 wrote could not be executed again and canonical
   BNG2 rejected it as well. The writer now emits the canonical BNGL spellings
   (`Arrhenius`, `Sat`, `Hill`, `MM`), which both BNG3 and BNG2 read.
2. The network compiler applied barrier patterns even with the experimental gate unset,
   while the capability report refused them for the network compiler without the gate. The
   network compiler now refuses barrier patterns with the gate unset and names it, so an
   experimental feature can never be active by default.

### CPU work-plan reconciliation and workload profile (#176)

`tests/validation/workload_profile.py` reconciles the recorded plan
(`docs/NFSIM_GPU_PERF_PLAN.md`) with the implemented surface and measures the workload
profile of the two execution modes.

The reconciliation reads the plan document and probes every one of its items: the
`benchmark_nfsim.py` harness of Track 0 (absent), the `canSkipIndirectMembership`
membership re-match of Track 1 (present), the pooled molecule arrays, the state-only
firing skip, the hierarchical selection, the faster random stream and the TDF dependency
tracking of Track 1 (absent), the `batch_size` on the NFsim action path of Track 2
(absent), the ensemble API the device backend has to honour (present, on the network-SSA
path) and the device kernels of Track 3 (present, for the batched network-SSA kernel
only). An item the build does not implement is recorded as absent: it is neither
presented as support nor as a product failure, and the roadmap priority stays a maintainer
decision.

The retained ensemble scope is pinned: seed `20261020`, a run budget of 256 trajectories
and a four-sigma band around the closed-form law of the case. The isolation properties are
executed rather than assumed: the same seed reproduces the batch bit for bit, and the
first members of a smaller batch equal the first members of a larger one, so every
trajectory owns its own stream.

The profile is measured with an A/A repeatability control and an independent correctness
control on every timed stage. A stage whose spread exceeds the noise limit is not
recorded as a result, so a noisy host produces no timing instead of an unreliable one.
The numbers are machine-specific and are recorded with the device, the environment and
the corpus.

The device result is supplementary. The accelerator inventory of this build is `metal`
(Apple M5 Pro, available) with `cuda` not compiled in, and the device kernel reproduces
the closed-form law of the network-SSA batch class. The device is claimed only for the
class this build ships a kernel for; the network-free NFsim event loop has no device
kernel and no ensemble API. The CPU-pinned contract
(`tests/python/test_ssa_closed_form_parity.py` and the CPU mode of
`tests/test_batch_ssa_statistical_parity.py`) remains the gated one.
